# SLICE-PLAN

Binding context: branch `arch/full-modularization-v1` from `kriya-pre-full-modularization-20261016` (= `a91157f`); Phase 0 is done. Every slice commits LOCALLY only (owner decision 1); nothing is pushed or merged before the single final independent review (Q2) and its one focused re-review. No `Co-Authored-By`. One slice = one Opus agent working only from these six documents + the code.

Fixed per-slice method (owner decision 12): characterize -> add architecture/behaviour tests first -> identify ownership -> extract with behaviour preserved -> focused tests -> (only in a correction slice) the authorized generic correction -> negative/mutation tests -> adjacent integration -> `.venv/bin/pylint kriya plugins/core_tools tests` and `.venv/bin/ruff check .` both exit 0 -> commit locally -> append one status line to §9 of this file (slice id, HEAD, tests run, tests NOT run) and regenerate `tests/architecture/baseline.json` when the slice changed an edge. Never combine a physical move, a rename, a semantic correction or unrelated cleanup in one commit. A slice that discovers a product defect outside its scope records it in `handover/BACKLOG_REGISTRY.csv` with `target_scope` = the slice that owns it (ENGINEERING_RULES §9 applies if it is in scope).

Size classes (one Opus context): **S** <= 3 production files and <= ~400 changed lines; **M** <= 6 production files and <= ~1,200 lines; **L** exactly one god-module seam, <= ~2,000 lines moved, no other production file beyond the new owner module and the facade. Anything bigger is split here already.

Field legend per slice: `goal` / `files` (new + changed) / `patch points & guards` (what must keep working or be rewritten in-slice) / `kind` (PRESERVE = behaviour-preserving, CORRECT = authorized behaviour change) / `stop-list` (plan §13 item; "owner-authorized" when covered by decisions 2-5) / `gate` (what runs first) / `tests` (added) / `mutants` / `commands` (Tier 1; Tier 2 per TEST-MATRIX §4) / `commit` / `deps` / `size`.

## 0. Owner directive 2 (2026-10-16) - BINDING; where a slice text below conflicts, this section wins

**Execution units.** The 64 slices are ACCEPTANCE units, each keeping its own coherent LOCAL commit, tests and status line; they are not 64 agent contexts. One Opus context may take adjacent slices that share an owning module and source context, with no independent semantic decision between them: A2+A3, B1+B2, B3+B4, C1+C2, D1+D2, J1+J2, K1+K2, L1+L2, M2+M3, O1+O2+O3, P1+P2. SEPARATE contexts (never batched, however adjacent) for: attribution (F), retry-target (F), candidate transactions (G), per-finding repair (G), budgets (H), fallback/routing (H), authority (C3 and any authority-semantics slice), provider/runtime seam (I). E-slices stay one seam per context unless two seams are in one file region with no decision between them.

**Order for every Scenario-04 semantic area (A-E):** characterization -> ownership extraction -> preservation proof -> semantic correction -> correction tests + mutants -> integration. No silent behaviour fix during extraction; a failing golden after an extraction slice means the extraction broke something.

**Budget (H2/H4).** Approved with one requirement: the 1800 s allowance is an explicit, documented default constant `HUMAN_WAIT_ALLOWANCE_SECONDS` owned by `workflow/budget_policy.py` and exposed as `autonomy.budgets.human_wait_allowance_seconds`; no other module contains that literal (a `test_kernel_purity`-style grep test enforces it). Keep distinct internally: ACTIVE inference budget; HUMAN WAIT elapsed; TOTAL RUN WALL-CLOCK; RUN LIVENESS CEILING; GATE/FALLBACK RESERVES. Required tests (H4): the recorded human wait still admits the fallback pass when active budget remains; active model work still exhausts the generation budget; human wait advances total wall clock; `run_liveness_seconds` terminates a genuinely hung/abandoned run (fake clock); liveness termination and generation-budget exhaustion are DIFFERENT typed reason codes with their own evidence. No new timeout abstraction unless evidence requires it.

**Attribution / retry target (F1-F5).**
- F1 first classifies EVERY current producer of `failure.authoritative_files` by PROVENANCE (MODULE-CONTRACTS `Provenance`), measured from the producing code, and finds every recorded failure carrying both an exact locator and a file set; the conflict rule is derived from provenance and pinned by a regression test from the real case. No rule from field names.
- F2 types `AttributionSource{provenance, authority_level, evidence_ref}` and `RetryTargetDecision` (unresolved findings, authoritative locator, advisory evidence, rule applied, selected, rejected candidates + why, origin original/expansion/fallback) as ONE recorded decision (`retry_target.decision`).
- F4 correction = expansion rule (MODULE-CONTRACTS §12): a model filename mention never replaces or overrides an unresolved deterministic locator; advisory file B may expand the target only after locator file A was staged, re-gated, and the same normalized failure signature remains, with B inside the legal write scope and no stronger deterministic contradiction; structural dependency evidence used when it exists. Six mandatory controls with tests+mutants: (1) bare mention cannot redirect; (2) a quoted compiler message naming another file cannot redirect; (3) the deterministic locator stays authoritative; (4) after A genuinely fails with the same signature, advisory B may be considered (the PRV-05 case); (5) B outside the allowed scope is refused; (6) a successful deterministic repair of A prevents any advisory redirect. This supersedes the weaker "locator exhaustion" wording of §4 decision 1.

**E14 (end of W4) completion report, mandatory.** Measure and record in SLICE-PLAN §9 and `CURRENT-DEPENDENCY-MAP.md`: `attempt.py` line count before (11,314) / after; responsibilities remaining in it; its direct imports before/after; state-mutation sites before/after; public entry points; cycles affected; owning test file per extracted concern. No line-count target, but a remaining ~10k-line orchestration module that still owns unrelated decisions means W4 is NOT complete: add E15+ slices until it is. Thin wrappers around an `attempt.py` that still owns every decision do not count.

**P1/P2.** Old physical paths are acceptable; old architectural dependencies are not. Forbidden direct imports gone; each facade removed or justified in `FACADES`; architecture tests protect each ownership.

**TEST-MATRIX §6 per-module speed record** (command, count, elapsed, network/model/process/container needs, contract and integration membership) is a deliverable filled by each module's last slice and completed by Q1.

**Q2 independent review standard.** The reviewer is told to find architectural reasons Kriya would need another major restructuring before adding providers, Skills/Tools or remote multi-user deployment, and explicitly to try to FALSIFY: "adding a remote multi-user backend later does not require another kernel restructuring". Mandatory probes: `Path.home` in business modules; process-global current user/project/run state; static singleton registries; global mutable configuration; global skills selection; global tool permissions; global evidence/history assumptions; provider requests without run/security context; application services coupled to the desktop transport. Also: god modules, hidden globals, cross-module state mutation, duplicate ownership, fake interfaces, transport/provider/filesystem leakage, untestable coupling, missing contract tests, architecture rules not mechanically enforced. BLOCKING and IMPORTANT fixed, then ONE focused re-review.

**Owner directive 3 (2026-10-16, after A1) - BINDING.**
- *Allowlist monotonicity.* The measured baseline (A1: 448 import-direction edges; A2: 11 direct-access + 10 kernel-purity entries = **469 tracked exceptions**) only shrinks. A slice may ADD an entry only when unavoidable and each new entry MUST state why it exists, the owning module and the exact removal slice (an existing entry moved to a new home with its removal slice unchanged is count-neutral and allowed). Q1 asserts: no allowlisted entry without a removal slice that exists in this plan; a removed entry cannot reappear (a `removed_edges` ledger in `tests/architecture/baseline.json`); no new unowned exception. The allowlist count is recorded after EVERY wave in the §9 ledger.
- *Characterization is not contract.* See TEST-MATRIX §3a: current wrong behaviour / why wrong / owning slice / final behaviour per A3 gate. The correction slice flips the pin, proves the old behaviour impossible, adds negative controls and its mutants.
- *Tripwires detect ownership, not paths.* The A2 finders are AST-based (calls, imports, attribute access), covering subprocess, git/worktree, `Path.home`/global state paths, environment and config reads, raw evidence/state DB access, direct provider selection, direct skill/tool filesystem access; bounded allowlists with removal slices.
- *W2 transport chain (do not alter).* Electron renderer -> typed IPC -> Electron main/host -> `kriya kup-stdio` child (reads/light writes; also `generate --launch-id` and `launch-decision`) -> KUP adapter -> `LocalKriyaClient` (Python) -> Application Services -> domain modules. KUP translates transport requests/responses ONLY (envelope, argument validation, error-code mapping); application services own use-case orchestration; domain modules own business decisions. Do not move business truth into KUP or into a service just to make the boundary easy. Raw sqlite/http/cwd/env/home access found inside a KUP body moves to its owning domain module (control/STATE, config/CONFIG) when B2/B3 extract the use case; where that cannot be done in the slice, the A2 allowlist entry MOVES WITH the code (kup -> application) keeping its removal slice (D2/D3), count-neutral, and the service takes no new direct-access site. `kriya/context` reads no env/home.
- *W2 completion check (B4 end-of-wave).* Before W2 is declared complete: (1) a real use case runs end to end through `LocalKriyaClient -> Application Service -> existing domain behaviour` (at minimum `execution.preflight` and `history.list`, plus the launch-decision approval path through `ApprovalService`) via a real `kup-stdio` child with byte-identical envelopes to `tests/golden/kup`, no semantic regression; (2) the B4 report lists: remaining business logic still in KUP, remaining CLI direct-domain access, every temporary compatibility path, each with its exact removal slice (O1/O2/P1...), and the allowlist count.

**Owner directive 4 (2026-10-17, QUALITY-FIRST) - BINDING.**
- *Priority:* QUALITY > ARCHITECTURAL CORRECTNESS > REPRODUCIBILITY > WALL-CLOCK > TOKEN COST. No deadline; the 90-130 h figure (21/67 slices done when measured, 46 left) is a planning estimate. Re-estimate from measured progress at E14, H5, P2 and Q1.
- *Batching ceiling = this validated plan.* No batching for E7-E14, none for F/G/H semantic work, the provider seam, state ownership or fallback; only the existing named groups in J, K, L, M, O, P (pairs and O1+O2+O3). Do not broaden silently. If in doubt, do not batch.
- *Gates serial from E8 on:* authoritative acceptance gates and the final canonical suite run serially; sandbox mutants and read-only analysis (AST/inventory) may run in parallel when isolated; lightweight independent checks may overlap only if they cannot affect interpretation. (E7 was verified serially as well.)
- *Per-slice orchestrator mutants stay mandatory* where planned (they found genuine gaps in E3, E4, E5, E6); they are not replaced by the final review.
- *Typed decisions:* AttributionDecision, RetryTargetDecision, BudgetDecision, FallbackDecision are in this plan; **CandidateTransactionDecision is defined deliberately in G2** (with `candidate_transaction.decision`): typed contract, evidence representation, tests, ownership, consistent with the other four. Not invented elsewhere. The I4 second-provider proof and the Skills/Tools health contracts stand and are not duplicated.
- *No green-by-weakening* (weaker assertions, unjustified wider allowlists, deleted coverage, broad normalization, suppression, expected-fail, scenario-specific exceptions, authority bypass, silent budget raises, changed goals, model judgment in place of deterministic evidence).
- *Push:* ONE normal non-force push of `arch/full-modularization-v1` is pre-authorized, only at final qualification and only after every condition holds (all slices complete; OPEN-ITEMS final gate clean; allowlists at their intended final state; module/contract/integration/architecture/static/UI/KUP gates green; independent review done; all BLOCKING and required IMPORTANT findings fixed; focused re-review passed; final serial canonical suite green in the certified environment; tracked tree clean; no unexpected untracked source; no force needed); then verify local HEAD == origin HEAD and create the annotated tag `kriya-v1.3.0-modularized-baseline`. No force push, no history rewrite, no intermediate or review-incomplete push, no other branch.
- *CLAUDE.md quota guidance is overridden for this workstream only* (CLAUDE.md is not edited): Opus workers and the planned spawns without per-spawn approval; no gratuitous agents.

**A1 carry-forward (from the A1 worker's report; code is ground truth).** `FACADES` must list `kriya/build_info.py` and `kriya/core/tomlcompat.py` (A2). `scan()` caches edges, not ASTs: add a cached `trees()` so each file is parsed once (A2). Allowlist keys are dotted module names. Some design §3 edges are now intra-module (`launch_approval -> runtime_command_approval`, `core.trace`, `tools.service -> mcp`, `agents -> workflow`): C3/D2/K1/E13 see those removals only as a smaller cycle baseline. B1 has 23 importers of `workspace_identity`, not 13. `KriyaError(reason_code, message)` is positional: subclasses must pass both. `metrics -> authority_approval` is in the P2 bucket although DEPENDENCY-RULES §3 called it permanent: decide in P2 (default: keep it as a declared, documented permanent edge only if `metrics` is reclassified as allowed to read config authority). TEST-MATRIX §1 has 9 marker families matching no file and several counts that differ (e.g. lr_r1_m1 24 vs 30): each module's last slice corrects its own families, Q1 asserts `every test file mapped`.

**Allowlist retarget (2026-10-16, before B3).** The three entries B2 moved into APPLICATION with the catch-all P2 now name their exact removal slice (count-neutral). None of the three slice texts names the surface, so each is an ASSIGNED work item for that slice: `application.history -> policy.telemetry` C1 (C1 creates `policy/authority.py`, the first public AUTHORITY facade: re-export `scrub_potential_secrets` from it); `application.history -> workflow.verification_authority` E9 (the runtime-verification seam that consumes `deterministic_sequence_kind`: put that classification on the KERNEL public surface); `application.project -> control.recovery` D2 (the slice that defines STATE's public surface: expose `assess_recovery` there).

## 1. Wave order (amends plan §9; justification in §7)

| Wave | Slices | Content | Wave check |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | A1, A2, A3 | inventory, architecture characterization, Scenario-04 gates | `-m architecture`, baseline committed |
| W2 | B1, B2, B3, B4 | context, application services built ONCE from the existing KUP/CLI bodies, KriyaClient/LocalKriyaClient, KUP as adapter | `-m "application or context or adapters"` |
| W3 | C1, C2, C3, D1, D2, D3, M1 | authority, evidence, state, config + the repository write/commit mechanism (breaks the control/policy/static_analysis cycles) | `-m "authority or evidence or state or config or repository"`, `-m architecture`: cycle baseline must shrink to the planned set |
| W4 | E1..E14, E14-P, E14-C | kernel/workflow decomposition (first-class, plan gap); E14-C = the owner-approved semantic correction of OI-55/OI-56, after the accepted E14 baseline (42d0e70; E14 PARTIAL, residue = E14R); E14-P = the owner-approved lazy-import startup correction of OI-61, after the accepted E14 baseline and before E14-C (69 slices in total since 2026-10-18; 70 with N1-CY, owner-approved 2026-10-19, in W9) | `-m kernel` once at wave end; `-m architecture` |
| W4b | R1, R2 | owner-approved corrections (2026-10-16) of defects the PRESERVE slices had to keep: atomic-write durability + temp cleanup (R1), one state-directory resolver (R2); between E14 and F1 | focused; `-m "kernel and state_machine"` | 
| W4c | HX1 | golden-harness portability micro-slice (OI-41), owner-approved 2026-10-17; after E4, before E5; harness ONLY, no product change | the 702+14 gate unchanged in outcome; sandbox calibration |
| W5 | F1..F5, G1..G5, H1..H5 | Scenario-04 corrections A-H in the mandatory order | `-m "kernel and state_machine"`, the three flipped gates |
| W6 | I1..I4 | provider runtime seam evolved | `-m provider`, `-m architecture` |
| W7 | J1, J2, K1, K2 | skills, knowledge, toolbox | `-m "skills or knowledge or tools"` |
| W8 | L1, L2, M2, M3 | execution port, repository port, direct subprocess/git removal | `-m "execution or repository"`, `-m architecture` |
| W9 | N1, N1-CY, N2 | qualification; N1-CY = the owner-approved (2026-10-19) Type D cycle-edge micro-slice, the 70th slice, between N1 and N2 | `-m qualification` |
| W10 | O1, O2, O3 | adapter migration (KUP, CLI, Electron chain) | `-m "adapters or application"`, `cd ui/kup && npm test` |
| W11 | P1, P2 | legacy removal, facades gone, allowlists empty | `-m architecture` |
| W12 | Q1, Q2 | enforcement final, overhead measurement, review package, Tier 3 canonical run, independent review + one re-review, tag | `scripts/run_full_suite.py` (owner) |

Wave check = the agent of the wave's last slice runs the wave's Tier 2 selectors (in background, once), confirms `-m architecture` green, regenerates `baseline.json`, and writes the wave line in §9. It is a lightweight self-check, NOT a review; the only independent review is Q2.

## 4. Owner decisions needed (not decided here; each has a recommendation and a default the slices implement if unanswered)

1. **Locator exhaustion rule (F4, PRV-05 compatibility).** Recommendation and default: a DERIVED_DETERMINISTIC locator is `exhausted` for a finding only when a candidate that changed the locator's file was STAGED and RE-GATED and the same failure signature recurred (a gate-level repeat); a response-validity failure (anchor, protocol, indentation) that collapsed onto the finding never exhausts it. PRV-05 (locator re-gated, same failure, explicit redirect in the analysis) still redirects; Scenario 04 attempt 2 (edit refused before any gate) does not. Alternative: never let MODEL_CLAIMED select a target (PRV-05 case would regress: not recommended).
2. **Budget field names/defaults (H4). AMENDED 2026-10-16 after primary-agent validation (the draft was wrong).** `generation_time_budget_seconds` KEEPS its meaning = total ACTIVE inference budget with HUMAN_WAIT excluded (it is today's cap in `llm.py::_deadline` and `attempt.py::_ensure_generation_time_budget`); the production preset's 900 stays that. The liveness ceiling is a NEW field `autonomy.budgets.run_liveness_seconds` (SEC-009-classified), unset -> derived = active budget + `human_wait_allowance_seconds` (default 1800). Per-phase fields (`planner_inference_seconds`, `developer_inference_seconds`, `repair_inference_seconds`, `verification_seconds`, `fallback_reserve_seconds`) default to None = share the total active budget (no per-phase cap unless set); `fallback_reserve_seconds` default = old `generation_gate_reserve_seconds` (120). No migration, no field removed. Reason for the amendment: with the old field as the wall-clock liveness cap the recorded Scenario-04 timeline (673 s elapsed, 300 s fallback pass, 900 s cap) would still fail, because the LLM deadline would kill the admitted pass. Alternative: hard per-phase defaults (changes every existing config: not recommended).
3. **Human wait and the liveness ceiling (H4). AMENDED.** Human wait counts toward LIVENESS only (wall clock) and toward no inference phase; the liveness ceiling is strictly larger than the total active budget by construction (derived default above; an explicit value <= the active budget is refused at config load with a typed reason). A human wait that exhausts the allowance stops the run with a typed liveness code rather than hanging (owner may change the allowance; no separate cap now).
4. **Independence proof basis (G4).** Recommendation/default: two proposals are independent when (a) no finding depends on both, (b) no code_intel symbol/type edge joins their files in either direction, (c) neither file is declared as the other's prerequisite by the plan's ownership chain; when code intelligence is unavailable the pair is `dependent` (atomic, today's behaviour). Alternative: file-level only (weaker; not recommended).
5. **Per-finding repair request shape (G4).** Recommendation/default: the Developer still receives one request per target file (the single-file contract stays), carrying every open finding for that file; a finding whose locators span files makes those files one atomic group requested in one attempt. Alternative: one request per finding across files (changes the Developer contract: not recommended now).
6. **Package naming.** Recommendation/default: `kriya/workflow` stays the kernel package; new packages `foundation`, `context`, `application`, `client`, `repository`, `toolbox`, `qualification`, `cli_commands`. Renaming `workflow` -> `kernel` would touch 166 test files for no ownership gain.
7. **H4 dependency on W6.** Recommendation/default: W5 precedes W6 (corrections first while the kernel context is fresh); H4 implements `TimingStore` in PROVIDER files and I2 adds the port method. Alternative: run W6 before W5 (then H4 is smaller).
8. **Locator vs. authoritative structural evidence on ONE failure (F4).** Today `failure.authoritative_files` (migration manifest evidence, PRV-05 run 7) is checked BEFORE the compiler locator (TRACED `attribution.py:1104-1112`); owner decision 3 ranks the compiler/validator locator first. Recommendation/default: keep decision 3's order; F1 must enumerate every recorded failure type that carries both a file:line locator and `authoritative_files` (INFERRED: none, `migration_incomplete` failures carry no locator), and if one exists, F4 treats the two as a conflict that selects the locator's file and records the structural file as `read_only_context`, with the owner informed in the F4 report. Alternative: keep today's order for that one pair (deviates from decision 3; not recommended without a measured case).

## 5. Risk register

| Risk | Mitigation baked into slices |
|---|---|
| A physical move silently breaks `mock.patch` strings / path guards (the brief's central concern) | per-module decision table (TARGET-MODULE-MAP §2); each moving slice lists the exact patch strings and guard paths it rewrites; `test_patch_points.py` (E1) + `test_facades.py` (A2); C facades only where <= 3 patch files |
| Kernel decomposition splits files but keeps hidden cross-dependencies | owner modules never import the facades (`test_patch_points` import rule), typed `*Deps`/`StageContext` only, `test_kernel_seams` call-through assertions, E1 golden evidence diffs per E-slice |
| Behaviour drift in W4 | PRESERVE-only slices, goldens, `-m "kernel and state_machine"` per slice, `-m kernel` once per E-slice in background, A3 gates must stay pinned until F4/G4/H4 |
| Corrections exceed the authorization (special-casing, retry policy change) | generic rules only (no Ignite/scenario strings: `test_kernel_purity` skill/goal-word table), fallback policy proven unchanged by `test_sm_reserved_fallback.py` mutant (H4-5), flips are explicit diffs of the A3 tests |
| New config fields widen authority | classification test + fail-closed default (`config/authority.py`), production preset floor kept |
| Evidence seal/readers break on new kinds | D1 round-trip + explain + retention tests before any emitter exists |
| Approval/authority regression during moves (C3, E4, E9, G4, M1) | `test_authority_invariants.py` reruns in every such slice with named mutants |
| Electron wire drift | O3 pin + `tests/test_kup_serve.py` cross-language assertion unchanged since B4; serve.py reloads config per request (kept) |
| Performance tax from the new layers | coarse service calls, no DI/locator, registries explicit, resolution once per run (J1 mutant), Q2 before/after measurement with a fixed budget: kup-stdio p50 latency +<= 10 ms, scripted run wall time +<= 3 % (INFERRED targets; measured in Q2) |
| Opus context overflow on a slice | L slices are single seams; P1 may split by package; every slice has its own gate + commands and needs no history |
| Docker/toolchain-dependent Tier 2 tests on the owner's machine | Docker tests stay pinned to the `docker` xdist group; javac-dependent assertions carry `real_toolchain`; the scripted parse/apply half runs everywhere |
| Allowlist stagnation | shrink-only lists with removal slice ids; P2 asserts empty; a stale entry fails |

## 6. Review checkpoints

- Per wave: the wave check (§1), recorded in §9. Not an independent review.
- Final: Q2's independent review (fresh agent; inspects code, dependency graph, architecture tests, representative tests; answers plan §15's 38 questions; BLOCKING/IMPORTANT fixed; one re-review of changed findings only). Only after that: the owner's Tier 3 run, the tag, and (owner) any push.

## 7. Amendments to the plan (with evidence)

1. **Wave order.** Plan §9 has no wave for kernel/workflow decomposition and none for corrections A-H; Wave 2 would wrap modules before they exist. Amended: W2 builds the application/client boundary ONCE over the existing operation bodies (`run_kup_operation` is already the dispatcher: TRACED `kup/cli_ops.py:42`, `kup/serve.py:4`), W4 = kernel decomposition (owner decision 6), W5 = corrections, W3 includes the repository write/commit mechanism because it is the cause of the control/policy/static_analysis -> workflow cycles (MEASURED, CURRENT-DEPENDENCY-MAP §3), not a "Wave 6" concern.
2. **Diagram.** Plan §5/§6.4 "Electron host -> LocalKriyaClient" is a cross-language edge. Real chain (TRACED, `kriya_service.ts`, `execution_host.ts:13,68`, `kriya_argv.ts:85-96`, `kup/serve.py`): renderer -> typed IPC -> Electron main -> child `kriya kup-stdio` (reads/light writes) | `kriya generate --launch-id` (run start) | `kriya launch-decision` (approvals) -> KUP/CLI adapter -> `LocalKriyaClient` -> services. Owner decision 7 confirms.
3. **Provider.** Plan §6.10/6.11 `ModelProvider` + registry: the seam exists (`InferenceRuntimePort`, `register_runtime_adapter`, MEASURED `core/inference_runtime.py:133,437-463`); evolved, not duplicated (owner decision 8).
4. **Tests.** Plan §10's directory layout is realized by markers + `tests/contracts/`, `tests/integration/`, `tests/architecture/` only (the plan allows "selectors/markers or equivalent"); relocating 562 files is pure risk.
5. **Push.** Plan §14 "push normally at stable checkpoints" is overridden by owner decision 1 (local only until the final review).
6. **Deliverables.** `INDEPENDENT-REVIEW.md` and `FINAL-VALIDATION.md` (plan §18) are produced by Q2, not by this design; `handover/KRIYA_ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md` gets one ADR entry per wave in the wave's last slice (plan §18: no competing ADR system).
7. **Self-diagnosis.** Plan §A "advisory unless no stronger evidence exists" is made precise by owner decision 3 + §4 decision 1 (exhaustion rule), so PRV-05 keeps passing.
8. **State directory.** Plan §6.8 "centralized at bootstrap/config ownership": realized by `config/environment.py` + `ConfigService` while `core/state_paths.py` stays physically (declared STATE; 64 test files).
9. **Brief corrections.** "core and tools do not import workflow": core does (one lazy edge) and tools reads the run clock from control (CURRENT-DEPENDENCY-MAP §2); both are allowlisted with their removal slices (N1, H2).
10. **"Kernel must not know state-directory filesystem details" (plan §6.5).** The kernel today builds `.kriya/` paths through `control.persistence` and reads `trace_db_path(cfg)`; after D2/D3 it receives `WorkspaceContext.state_dir` and store objects; the remaining `.kriya/` path construction is owned by STATE/REPOSITORY (`test_direct_access_owners` table).

11. **Zero-known-gap completion (owner decisions 2026-10-19).** The plan below is extended by the correction units in `ZERO-KNOWN-GAP-UNITS.md` (45 proposed units, owner-cohesive; R3 / R4 / R5 confirmed by the owner; E14 PARTIAL with the residue units E14R, E14X, E15). Slice total 70 + 45 = 115 before the P2 preflight outcome; the count is not frozen. The evidence is in `REGISTRY-TRIAGE/`.

## 8. Stop-list summary

Slices flagged (all owner-authorized by decisions 2-5, implemented generically): F4 (retry behaviour, user-visible), G4 (retry behaviour, user-visible), H4 (user-visible: budgets; new config fields; retry trigger count proven unchanged). No slice changes authority/security semantics, requirement-ledger semantics, performs a destructive migration, removes validated behaviour or adds server infrastructure; C3/E4/E9/M1 touch authority CODE location only and rerun the C1 invariants with mutants.
